  • I3C Core Controller

I3C Core Controller

1. Overview

The OCA I3C Core Controller is a comprehensive MIPI I3C/I2C communication controller providing full HCI (Host Controller Interface) v1.2 compliant operation with support for both controller (master) and target (slave) modes. Based on the CHIPS Alliance i3c-core with extensive OCA enhancements, this controller enables high-speed inter-chip communication with advanced features including In-Band Interrupts, Common Command Codes, and device recovery.

2. Description and Features

  • MIPI Specification Compliance: Full compliance with I3C Basic v1.0/v1.1.1, I3C HCI v1.2, and TCRI v1.0 recovery specifications

  • Dual Operating Modes: Configurable as active controller, secondary controller, or I3C target with runtime mode switching

  • High-Speed Operation: Push-Pull mode up to 12.5 MHz, Open-Drain mode for I2C compatibility (100 kHz to 1 MHz)

  • Advanced Controller Features: Host Controller Interface with descriptor-based queue management, threshold-triggered interrupts, DAT/DCT device tables

  • In-Band Interrupts (IBI): Hardware-managed IBI detection and handling with priority-based acceptance, reduced-latency OCA enhancement for IBI during transactions

  • Common Command Codes (CCC): Full support for broadcast and direct CCCs including dynamic address assignment (SETDASA, SETNEWDA), device configuration (SETMWL, SETMRL), and device queries (GETPID, GETSTATUS)

  • I2C Backward Compatibility: Standard-mode (100 kbaud), Fast-mode (400 kbaud), and Fast-mode Plus (1 Mbaud) support for legacy I2C devices

  • Transfer Command Response Interface (TCRI): MIPI TCRI v1.0 recovery handler with firmware download and peripheral reset capabilities

  • Multi-Instance Support: Up to 6 independent I3C controller instances with unified register interface

  • Flexible Queue Architecture: Separate Command, Response, TX, RX, and IBI FIFOs with configurable depths and threshold-based operation

  • AXI4-Lite Integration: Standard 32-bit AXI4-Lite slave interface for register access with address space multiplexing

3. Architecture

The I3C Core Controller is a MIPI I3C v1.0/v1.1.1 and HCI v1.2 compliant communication controller supporting both active controller (master) and target (slave) operations with I2C backward compatibility. The architecture provides high reliability with hardware-managed queue operations, threshold-based interrupts, and AXI4-Lite integration.

3.1. System Overview

The I3C controller is organized as a three-level wrapper hierarchy:

  • i3ccore_wrapper: Top-level multi-instance demultiplexer supporting up to 6 independent I3C instances with AXI4-Lite address decoding

  • i3c_wrapper: Per-instance wrapper handling CSR interfacing and signal routing for a single I3C controller

  • i3c: Main I3C module containing HCI queues, control/status registers, controller/target FSMs, and physical layer interface

Each I3C instance occupies 0x1000 bytes of address space (12-bit addressing), allowing software to access multiple controllers through a unified AXI4-Lite interface.

This document goes over the i3c controller on a high level. For detailed i3c controller fsm overview, look at the i3c-core-fork repo, under: doc/i3c_controller_fsm_specification.adoc === Block Diagram

The I3C controller consists of the following major functional blocks:

                              +------------------+
                              |   Software       |
                              |   (CPU/DMA)      |
                              +---------+--------+
                                        |
                                 AXI4-Lite Bus
                                        |
                              +---------v--------+
                              | i3ccore_wrapper  |
                              | (Address Demux)  |
                              +--+----+----+----++
                                 |    |    |    |
                        Instance 0..1..2...5
                                 |
                         +-------v---------+
                         |  i3c_wrapper    |
                         |  (Per Instance) |
                         +-------+---------+
                                 |
                      +----------v----------+
                      |      i3c.sv         |
                      |  (Main Controller)  |
                      +---------------------+
                      |                     |
                      |  I3CCSR (Register)  |
                      |                     |
                      +----------+----------+
                                 |
        +------------------------+------------------------+
        |                        |                        |
+-------v--------+      +--------v-------+       +--------v--------+
|  HCI Queues    |      |  Controller    |       |    I3C PHY      |
|  - Command     |      |  - flow_active |       | (CDC Sync)      |
|  - Response    |<---->|  - ctrl_active |<----->|                 |
|  - TX Data     |      |  - ctrl_fsm    |       | SCL/SDA I/O     |

+----------------+      +--------+-------+       +-----------------+
                                 |
                        +--------v--------+
                        |   DAT / DCT     |
                        | (Device Tables) |
                        +-----------------+

3.2. Functional Blocks

3.3. Control and Status Registers (I3CCSR)

The I3CCSR module provides a comprehensive register interface organized into hierarchical sections:

  • I3CBase (0x00-0x68): Core controller configuration including HC_CONTROL, PRESENT_STATE, INTR_STATUS, RESET_CONTROL, and section offset pointers

  • PIOControl (0x80-0xB0): Programmable I/O interface with command/response/data ports and threshold configuration registers

  • I3C_EC (Extended Capabilities, 0x100-0x3A0): Secure Firmware Recovery Interface (0x100-0x168), Standby Controller Mode (0x180-0x1C0), Target Transaction Interface (0x200-0x290), SoC Management Interface (0x300-0x390), Controller Config (0x398-0x39C), and Termination (0x3A0)

  • DAT (0x400-0x7FF): Device Address Table, 128-entry address window (2 DWORDs per entry) mapping device indexes to dynamic addresses

  • DCT (0x800-0xFFF): Device Characteristic Table, 128-entry address window (4 DWORDs per entry) holding device-specific characteristics

All registers are 32-bit aligned and accessed via a 12-bit address interface from the AXI4-Lite slave.

3.4. HCI Queue Management

The Host Controller Interface implements five independent FIFO structures for descriptor-based transaction management:

Command Queue (64-bit entries): Software writes command descriptors specifying transaction type (private transfer, CCC, I2C), target address, data length, and control flags (TOC, WROC, RnW). The queue depth is configurable via RDL parameters.

Response Queue (32-bit entries): Hardware generates response descriptors upon transaction completion containing transaction ID, error code, and actual bytes transferred. Response ready triggers RESP_READY interrupt.

TX Data Queue (32-bit entries): Stores outgoing data for controller write transactions. Software fills the queue triggered by TX_THLD_STAT interrupt when free space exceeds threshold. Hardware fetches data in 4-byte increments during write operations.

RX Data Queue (32-bit entries): Stores incoming data from controller read transactions and target mode receives. Hardware accumulates received bytes in 4-byte chunks and pushes to queue. RX_THLD_STAT interrupt triggered when data available exceeds threshold.

IBI Queue (32-bit entries): Stores In-Band Interrupt descriptors containing IBI source address, optional payload data, and timestamp. IBI_STATUS_THLD interrupt triggered when IBI entries exceed threshold.

Unified Queue Design: When both CONTROLLER_SUPPORT and TARGET_SUPPORT are enabled, TX and RX queues are shared between controller and target modes to reduce area. The OPERATION_MODE register selects which mode can access the queues at runtime.

3.5. I3C Controller Processing Engine

The controller processing engine manages all I3C protocol operations through integrated state machines coordinating byte-level (flow_active.sv) and bit-level (i3c_controller_fsm.sv) operations.

flow_active.sv (Transaction Flow FSM): Orchestrates high-level transaction flow including command descriptor parsing, FIFO fetching/pushing, byte-by-byte transfer sequencing, DAT/DCT lookup, and response descriptor generation. Handles private transfers, CCC commands, and IBI reception.

i3c_controller_fsm.sv (Physical Layer FSM): Manages bit-level SCL/SDA timing control for START/STOP generation, byte transmission with T-bit parity, ACK/NACK detection, and mode switching between Open-Drain and Push-Pull. Uses software-configured timing parameters for precise bus timing across all clock frequencies.

The controller FSM supports two timing modes:

Timing Parameter Open-Drain Mode Push-Pull Mode

Rise Time (t_r)

Programmable (typ. 1000ns I2C)

0-50ns

Fall Time (t_f)

Programmable (typ. 300ns I2C)

0-50ns

SCL High (thigh)

Programmable (≥4000ns I2C-SM)

Programmable

SCL Low (tlow)

Programmable (≥4700ns I2C-SM)

Programmable

Data Setup (tsu_dat)

Programmable (≥250ns I2C)

Programmable

Data Hold (thd_dat)

Programmable (≥0ns I2C)

Programmable

START Hold (thd_sta)

Programmable (≥4000ns I2C)

N/A

START Setup (tsu_sta)

Programmable (≥4700ns I2C)

N/A

STOP Setup (tsu_sto)

Programmable (≥4000ns I2C)

N/A

Bus Free (t_buf)

Programmable (≥4700ns I2C)

N/A

Open-Drain mode uses slower timing compatible with I2C and multi-controller arbitration. Push-Pull mode achieves 12.5 MHz operation with near-zero rise/fall times for maximum I3C performance.

3.6. High-Level Transaction Flows

All I3C transactions follow a common initialization sequence before diverging into transaction-specific operations. The flows below trace data movement from software commands through HCI queues, flow control FSM, physical layer FSM, and finally to the I3C bus.

3.7. Overall Architecture Data Flow

 +-----------+
 | Software  |
 |  (CPU)    |
 +-----------+
      |
      | AXI4-Lite Write/Read
      v
 +--------------------+
 |  i3ccore_wrapper   |
 | (Address Demux)    |
 +--------------------+
      |
      | CSR Interface
      v
 +--------------------+
 |   I3CCSR (Regs)    |
 | - COMMAND_PORT     |
 | - TX_DATA_PORT     |
 | - RX_DATA_PORT     |
 | - RESPONSE_PORT    |
 | - IBI_PORT         |
 +--------------------+
      |
      | reg2hw / hw2reg
      v
 +--------------------+
 |   HCI Queue Layer  |
 | +----------------+ |
 | | Command Queue  | |
 | | Response Queue | |
 | | TX Data Queue  | |
 | | RX Data Queue  | |
 | | IBI Queue      | |
 | +----------------+ |
 +--------------------+
      |
      | Queue interfaces
      v
 +--------------------+       +-------------+
 |  flow_active.sv    |<----->|  DAT / DCT  |
 | (Transaction FSM)  |       | (Lookup)    |
 | - Parse commands   |       +-------------+
 | - Manage byte flow |
 | - Generate response|
 +--------------------+
      |
      | TX/RX byte + control
      v
 +--------------------+
 | i3c_controller_fsm |
 | (Physical Layer)   |
 | - SCL/SDA timing   |
 | - START/STOP/ACK   |
 | - T-bit parity     |
 +--------------------+
      |
      | SCL/SDA signals
      v
 +--------------------+
 |    i3c_phy.sv      |
 |  (CDC Sync)        |
 |  OD/PP Mode Select |
 +--------------------+
      |
      v
   I3C Bus
  (SCL/SDA)

Software writes commands and data via AXI4-Lite to I3CCSR registers mapped to HCI queues. The flow_active FSM monitors queue status and initiates transactions by fetching command descriptors and coordinating with i3c_controller_fsm for bit-level bus control. Device characteristics are looked up from DAT/DCT tables. Responses and received data flow back through queues to software via interrupt-driven or polled access.

3.8. Transaction Initialization Flow

Every controller-initiated transaction begins with a common initialization sequence:

   [START]
      |
      v
  +--------+
  |  IDLE  |<----------------------+
  +--------+                       |
      |                           |
      | Command Available         |
      v                           |
 +--------------+                 |
 | WaitStartTrig|                 |
 +--------------+                 |
      |                           |
      | TX/RX Threshold Met       |
      v                           |
 +-----------+                    |
 | FetchDAT  |                    |
 +-----------+                    |
      |                           |
      | DAT Entry Captured        |
      v                           |
 +---------------+                |
 | BroadcastAddr |                |
 |  Send 0x7E+W  |                |
 +---------------+                |
      |                           |
      +---> [Transaction Phase] --+
            (Write/Read/CCC/IBI)

  1. IDLE: flow_active.sv waits for command descriptor availability in Command Queue (signaled by hardware queue depth tracking)

  2. WaitStartTrig: Check TX/RX FIFO threshold conditions to ensure sufficient data/space before starting transaction (prevents underflow/overflow)

  3. FetchDAT: Look up target device dynamic address from Device Address Table using dev_idx field from command descriptor. DAT provides the dynamic address, IBI accept/reject configuration, and auto-command settings.

  4. BroadcastAddr: Transmit I3C broadcast address (0x7E) + Write bit + T-bit in Open-Drain mode. Purpose: Alert all targets that a transaction is starting and allow multi-controller arbitration. This phase is mandatory for all controller-initiated I3C transactions.

  5. Transition to transaction-specific phase based on command type (private write/read, CCC, or IBI detection during broadcast).

3.9. Controller Write Transaction Flow

  From BroadcastAddr
         |
         v
  +---------------+
  |  TargetAddr   |
  | Send Addr+W+Sr|
  +---------------+
         |
         +--> NACK --> WriteResp --> IDLE
         |             (Error)
         v ACK
  +--------------+
  | FetchTxData  |
  | Read TX FIFO |
  +--------------+
         |
         +--> TX Empty --> WriteResp --> IDLE
         |                 (Error)
         v Data Available
  +---------------+
  | I3CDataWrite  |
  | Send byte+Tbit|<--+
  +---------------+   |
         |            |
         | Every 4B   |
         +------------+
         |
         v Transfer Complete
  +-------------+
  |  WriteResp  |
  | Gen Response|
  +-------------+
         |
         v
      IDLE

Detailed Step-by-Step Flow:

  1. TargetAddr: Transmit target device 7-bit dynamic address + R/W=0 (write) + Sr (repeated START). If target supports Push-Pull mode (indicated in DCT), switch from Open-Drain to Push-Pull for improved speed. Wait for ACK (I2C) or T-bit (I3C) acknowledgment from target.

  2. FetchTxData: Read 32-bit DWORD (4 bytes) from TX Data FIFO. Software must have previously filled TX FIFO in response to TX_THLD_STAT interrupt, which fires when free FIFO space ≥ TX_BUF_THLD bytes.

  3. I3CDataWrite: Transmit data bytes sequentially:

    • Send byte MSB-first (bit 7 → bit 0) on SDA

    • Generate T-bit (odd parity for I3C: T = byte XOR parity)

    • Fetch new DWORD from TX FIFO every 4 bytes

    • Repeat until data_length bytes transferred from command descriptor

  4. WriteResp: Generate response descriptor containing:

    • Transaction ID (TID from command descriptor)

    • Error code (SUCCESS, NACK, UNDERFLOW, etc.)

    • Actual bytes transferred

    • Push descriptor to Response Queue

  5. Trigger INTR_STATUS.RESP_READY interrupt to notify software

  6. Return to IDLE state for next transaction

Error Handling: * NACK on TargetAddr → Abort, generate error response (ERROR_NACK) * TX FIFO Empty during FetchTxData → Abort, generate error response (ERROR_UNDERFLOW)

Producer-Consumer Pattern: Software (producer) fills TX FIFO triggered by threshold interrupts, hardware (consumer) fetches data during transmission. Threshold-based operation prevents FIFO underflow while minimizing interrupt overhead.

3.10. Controller Read Transaction Flow

  From BroadcastAddr
         |
         v
  +---------------+
  |  TargetAddr   |
  | Send Addr+R+Sr|
  +---------------+
         |
         +--> NACK --> WriteResp --> IDLE
         |             (Error)
         v ACK
  +---------------+
  | I3CDataRead   |<-------+
  | Recv byte     |        |
  +---------------+        |
         |                 |
         v                 |
  +---------------+        |
  |  PushRxData   |        |
  | Write RX FIFO |        |
  +---------------+        |
         |                 |
         +--> Full --> WriteResp --> IDLE
         |             (Error)
         |                 |
         +-- More Data ----+
         |   (loop every 4B)
         v Transfer Complete
  +-------------+
  |  WriteResp  |
  | Gen Response|
  +-------------+
         |
         v
      IDLE

Detailed Step-by-Step Flow:

  1. TargetAddr: Transmit target device 7-bit dynamic address + R/W=1 (read) + Sr. Switch to Push-Pull if target supports it. Wait for ACK/T-bit from target confirming readiness to send data.

  2. I3CDataRead: Receive data bytes from target:

    • Sample SDA bit-by-bit (MSB first) on SCL rising edge

    • Send ACK after each byte to request more data

    • Send NACK after final byte to terminate read

    • Accumulate received bytes into 32-bit DWORD

  3. PushRxData: Write accumulated DWORD (4 bytes) to RX Data FIFO. Controller waits if RX FIFO full. Software must drain RX FIFO in response to RX_THLD_STAT interrupt, which fires when available data ≥ RX_BUF_THLD bytes.

  4. Loop I3CDataRead ↔ PushRxData until data_length bytes received from command descriptor

  5. WriteResp: Generate response descriptor with transfer status and push to Response Queue

  6. Trigger INTR_STATUS.RESP_READY interrupt

  7. Return to IDLE

Error Handling: * NACK on TargetAddr → Abort with ERROR_NACK * RX FIFO Full during PushRxData → Abort with ERROR_OVERFLOW

Consumer-Producer Pattern: Target (producer) transmits data, controller accumulates and pushes to RX FIFO, software (consumer) drains FIFO triggered by threshold interrupts.

3.11. In-Band Interrupt (IBI) Flow

I3C In-Band Interrupts enable targets to asynchronously notify the controller without requiring a separate interrupt pin. The OCA implementation supports two IBI entry mechanisms:

    Entry 1: From IDLE      Entry 2: From BroadcastAddr
    (Timer Expiry)          (During Transaction - OCA Enhancement)
           |                            |
           +----------+-----------------+
                      |
                      v
              +----------------+
              | ReadTargetAddr |
              | Identify IBI   |
              | Source         |
              +----------------+
                      |
                      +--> Address Invalid --> WriteResp --> Resume/IDLE
                      |    or IBI Disabled     (Error)
                      v Address OK
              +----------------+
              |  I3CDataRead   |<-------+
              | Recv IBI Data  |        |
              +----------------+        |
                      |                 |
                      v                 |
              +----------------+        |
              |  PushRxData    |        |
              | Write IBI FIFO |        |
              +----------------+        |
                      |                 |
                      +--> Full --> WriteResp --> Resume/IDLE
                      |             (Error)
                      |                 |
                      +-- More Data ----+
                      |   (<255 bytes)
                      v Data Complete
              +----------------+
              |   WriteResp    |
              | IBI Status Desc|
              +----------------+
                      |
                      v
         +------------+------------+
         |                         |
    Transaction              Return to
    Interrupted?                IDLE
         |
    Resume at
    saved state

IBI Entry Mechanism 1: Bus Available Timer (Standard)

  1. Bus goes idle (no controller activity)

  2. Target’s bus available timer (configured via CSR) begins counting

  3. Timer expires and target checks if bus still idle

  4. Target initiates IBI by driving SDA low (START condition)

  5. Controller detects START on idle bus → IBI detected

IBI Entry Mechanism 2: Interrupt During Transaction (OCA Enhancement)

This mechanism is unique to the OCA implementation and significantly reduces IBI response latency:

  1. Controller transmits broadcast address (0x7E) in Open-Drain mode at start of transaction

  2. Target with pending IBI drives its own address onto SDA simultaneously during broadcast transmission

  3. Controller detects address mismatch on SDA (wired-OR conflict) → IBI detected

  4. Controller aborts current transaction, saves state, and immediately services IBI

  5. After IBI completion, controller resumes interrupted transaction from saved state

Benefit: OCA’s implementation allows targets to interrupt ongoing transactions rather than waiting for bus idle, reducing IBI response latency by up to milliseconds in active systems.

IBI Processing Flow:

  1. ReadTargetAddr: Read 7-bit IBI source address from SDA

    • Verify address exists in DAT (valid target)

    • Check DAT.IBI_REJECT flag (IBIs from this device are NACKed when set)

  2. Decision - Accept or Reject IBI:

    • Accept (ACK): DAT.IBI_REJECT=0 AND DAT.IBI_PAYLOAD=1 AND IBI Queue not full

    • Reject (NACK): DAT.IBI_REJECT=1 OR DAT.IBI_PAYLOAD=0 OR IBI Queue full

  3. I3CDataRead (if accepted): Receive optional IBI payload

    • DAT.IBI_PAYLOAD indicates the device sends a mandatory data byte (e.g., sensor threshold value)

    • Maximum 255 bytes per I3C spec

  4. PushRxData: Write IBI data to IBI FIFO in 4-byte chunks

  5. WriteResp: Create IBI status descriptor containing:

    • IBI source address (7-bit)

    • Payload length (0-255 bytes)

    • Timestamp

    • Push to IBI_PORT (0x8C)

  6. Trigger INTR_STATUS.IBI_STATUS_THLD interrupt when IBI entries ≥ threshold

  7. Resume or Return:

    • If IBI interrupted transaction (Mechanism 2): Resume at saved state

    • Otherwise: Return to IDLE

  8. Software reads IBI descriptor from IBI_PORT and determines appropriate response (query target, send CCC, etc.)

3.12. Common Command Codes (CCC) Flow

Common Command Codes enable the controller to configure targets, query device information, and manage bus topology. CCCs are classified as broadcast (affecting all targets) or direct (targeting specific device).

    From BroadcastAddr
           |
           v
    +----------------+
    | CCC_SendCCCCode|
    | Send CCC byte  |
    +----------------+
           |
           v
    [Has Defining Byte?]
           |
    +------+------+
    |             |
  Yes            No
    |             |
    v             v
 +-------+    [Continue]
 | DefByte|       |
 +-------+       |
    |             |
    +------+------+
           |
           v
    [CCC Type Decision]
           |
    +------+------+------+------+
    |      |      |      |      |
 Bcast  Bcast  Direct Direct  Addr
 Write  Write  Write   Read  Assign
 (NoDef)(Def)                (SETDASA)
    |      |      |      |      |
    |      v      v      v      v
    |   Fetch  Target Target  Special
    |   TxData  Addr   Addr   Sequence
    |      |      |      |      |
    |      v      v      v      |
    |   I3C    I3C    I3C      |
    |   Write  Write  Read     |
    |      |      |      |      |
    +------+------+------+------+
           |
           v
      WriteResp
           |
           v
        IDLE

Broadcast CCC (e.g., ENEC, DISEC):

  1. CCC_SendCCCCode: Transmit CCC command byte (e.g., 0x00 for ENEC - Enable Events Command) + T-bit

  2. CCC_DefiningByte (optional, rare): Transmit defining byte for CCCs requiring sub-command selection (e.g., ENEC with specific event enables). Most CCCs skip this step.

  3. WriteResp: No target response expected for broadcast CCCs. All targets process command in parallel via their ccc.sv module. Generate success response.

  4. Return to IDLE

Direct Write CCC (e.g., SETMWL, SETMRL):

  1. CCC_SendCCCCode: Transmit CCC command byte

  2. TargetAddr: Transmit target device address + Sr

  3. FetchTxData: Retrieve CCC-specific data from TX FIFO

    • SETMWL: 2 bytes (max write length in bytes)

    • SETMRL: 2 bytes (max read length in bytes)

    • Data prepared by software and written to TX_DATA_PORT

  4. I3CDataWrite: Transmit CCC data bytes to target

  5. WriteResp: Generate response descriptor

  6. Return to IDLE

Direct Read CCC (e.g., GETPID, GETMWL, GETSTATUS):

  1. CCC_SendCCCCode: Transmit CCC command byte with read bit set (e.g., 0x8D for GETPID)

  2. TargetAddr: Transmit target address + Sr + Read bit

  3. I3CDataRead: Receive CCC response data from target

    • GETPID: 6 bytes (48-bit Provisioned ID)

    • GETMWL: 2 bytes (max write length)

    • GETSTATUS: 2 bytes (device status flags)

  4. PushRxData: Write response data to RX FIFO

  5. WriteResp: Generate response descriptor with data length

  6. Software reads CCC response from RX_DATA_PORT

  7. Return to IDLE

Address Assignment CCC (SETDASA):

  1. CCC_SendCCCCode: Transmit SETDASA command (0x87) or SETNEWDA (0x88)

  2. I3CAddressAssignment: Special two-address sequence:

    • Transmit Repeated START (Sr)

    • Transmit static address (7-bit, from device datasheet) + T-bit

    • Transmit dynamic address to assign (7-bit, chosen by controller) + T-bit

    • Transmit STOP

  3. Target with matching static address accepts assigned dynamic address and stores internally

  4. WriteResp: Confirm address assignment success

  5. Controller updates DAT memory with new dynamic address mapping

  6. Return to IDLE

CCC Data Sourcing: CCC-specific data originates from two sources: * Internal HCI settings: Hardware-provided values (e.g., max write length from HC_CAPABILITIES register) * TX FIFO: Software-provided values (e.g., custom configuration data)

Defining Byte Usage: Rarely used - only for CCCs requiring sub-command selection (e.g., ENEC/DISEC with event type mask). Most CCCs proceed directly to target addressing or broadcast without defining byte.

3.13. Target Mode Operation

When TARGET_SUPPORT=1, the I3C controller can operate as a target device responding to controller commands. Target functionality includes:

i3c_target_fsm.sv: Target mode state machine monitoring bus for address matches, processing received commands, and generating responses.

CCC Processing: The ccc.sv module (44 KB) implements comprehensive CCC command processing including: * SETDASA/SETNEWDA: Accept dynamic address assignment * GETPID/GETBCR/GETDCR: Respond with device identification * SETMWL/SETMRL: Configure max transfer lengths * ENEC/DISEC: Enable/disable event generation (IBI, hot-join)

Target Transaction Interface (TTI): When enabled via I3C_EC.TTI registers, provides descriptor-based RX/TX queues for target mode data transfers, mirroring controller mode queue architecture.

IBI Generation: Target can initiate IBI when enabled via ENEC CCC, subject to bus available timer or immediate transmission capability during controller broadcast.

3.14. Device Address Table (DAT) and Device Characteristic Table (DCT)

DAT (Device Address Table): 64-bit entries mapping device indexes to dynamic addresses. Each entry contains: * DYNAMIC_ADDRESS[23:16]: dynamic address; only the low 7 bits are driven onto the bus, hardware appends RnW and computes the ENTDAA parity bit * STATIC_ADDRESS[6:0]: static address (for SETDASA correlation) * IBI_REJECT[13]: NACK IBIs from this device (active high - clear to accept) * IBI_PAYLOAD[12]: device sends a mandatory data byte with its IBI * CRR_REJECT[14], TS[15], RING_ID[28:26], DEV_NACK_RETRY_CNT[30:29], DEVICE[31] * AUTOCMD_MASK/VALUE/MODE/HDR_CODE[58:32]: auto-command configuration

Software populates DAT during device enumeration. Hardware reads DAT during command processing to translate dev_idx to target address.

DCT (Device Characteristic Table): 128-bit entries with device-specific characteristics: * PID_HI[31:0] / PID_LO[47:32]: 48-bit Provisioned ID * DCR[71:64]: Device Characteristics Register - device type classification * BCR[79:72]: Bus Characteristics Register - device role, limitations * DYNAMIC_ADDRESS[103:96]: address assigned during ENTDAA

Hardware populates DCT during ENTDAA from the PID/BCR/DCR each target emits.

Depth: both tables expose a 128-entry address window, and DAT_SECTION_OFFSET / DCT_SECTION_OFFSET advertise TABLE_SIZE=127, but the physical memories are DAT_DEPTH/DCT_DEPTH = 16 entries deep (i3c_defines.svh). Only indexes 0-15 are independent. Higher indexes alias modulo 16 because the memory index is four bits wide; accessing slot 16 reads or overwrites slot 0. Software must not allocate additional devices based on the advertised table size.

The SoC Management Interface capability length is also inconsistent with the implemented next-header address. See Memory Map for the exact boundary and table-aliasing limitations.

3.15. PHY Layer and Clock Domain Crossing

i3c_phy.sv: Provides dual flip-flop CDC synchronization for SCL/SDA inputs crossing from I3C bus clock domain to controller system clock domain. Implements sel_od_pp control for Open-Drain vs Push-Pull pad mode selection.

Timing Flexibility: All bus timing parameters (t_r, t_f, thigh, tlow, tsu_*, thd_*, t_buf) are software-programmable via CSR registers, enabling operation across arbitrary system clock frequencies. Controller FSM generates precise SCL/SDA edges by counting system clock cycles derived from configured timing values.

3.16. Transfer Command Response Interface (TCRI)

The MIPI TCRI v1.0 recovery subsystem enables secure firmware download and device recovery:

recovery_handler.sv: Coordinates recovery flow state machine recovery_executor.sv: Executes recovery commands recovery_transmitter.sv / recovery_receiver.sv: Handle recovery data transfers recovery_pec.sv: Packet Error Code calculation for data integrity

Recovery interface signals (recovery_payload_available_o, recovery_image_activated_o, peripheral_reset_o, escalate_reset_o) connect to system-level reset and firmware management infrastructure.

3.17. Error Handling

The controller detects and reports multiple error conditions via response descriptors and interrupt flags:

  • Protocol Errors: NACK on address, CRC failure (HDR modes), frame errors

  • FIFO Errors: TX underflow, RX overflow, IBI queue overflow

  • Timeout Errors: Bus timeout, clock stretching timeout

  • Abort Conditions: Software abort via HC_CONTROL.ABORT, bus arbitration loss

Error codes in response descriptors enable software to implement retry logic, device blacklisting, or system-level error escalation.

3.18. Interrupt System

The controller generates interrupts from multiple sources:

  • Queue Thresholds: CMD_QUEUE_READY, RESP_READY, TX_THLD_STAT, RX_THLD_STAT, IBI_STATUS_THLD

  • Transfer Events: TRANSFER_COMPLETE, TRANSFER_ABORT, TRANSFER_ERROR

  • IBI Events: IBI_UPDATED, IBI_THLD_STAT

Software configures interrupt enables via INTR_STATUS_ENABLE (mask) and INTR_SIGNAL_ENABLE (routing to interrupt output pin). Reading INTR_STATUS provides current interrupt state; writing 1 to status bits clears interrupts.

Threshold-based interrupts implement producer-consumer flow control, triggering software action only when queue fill/empty levels cross configured thresholds, minimizing interrupt overhead compared to per-byte polling.

4. Memory Map

4.1. Overview

The I3C Core Controller provides a comprehensive register interface organized into five major sections accessible through an AXI4-Lite slave interface. The register space is organized into a 12-bit address space (0x000-0xFFF) per I3C instance, with each instance occupying 0x1000 bytes of address space when multiple instances are implemented via i3ccore_wrapper.

The register map follows the MIPI I3C HCI v1.2 specification with OCA-specific extensions for recovery and enhanced IBI handling. All registers are 32-bit aligned and support only 32-bit access widths.

4.2. Register Organization

  • I3CBase Section (0x00-0x68): Core controller configuration and status registers including bus control, capabilities, interrupts, and reset management

  • PIOControl Section (0x80-0xB0): Programmable I/O interface with queue ports, threshold controls, and PIO-specific interrupt management

  • I3C_EC Section (0x100-0x3A0): Extended Capabilities including Secure Firmware Recovery Interface (0x100-0x168), Standby Controller Mode (0x180-0x1C0), Target Transaction Interface (0x200-0x290), SoC Management Interface (0x300-0x390, includes bus-timing registers), Controller Config (0x398-0x39C), and Termination (0x3A0)

  • DAT Section (0x400-0x7FF): Device Address Table, 128-entry address window (2 DWORDs / 64-bit per entry); physical depth DAT_DEPTH=16 (usable index 0-15)

  • DCT Section (0x800-0xFFF): Device Characteristic Table, 128-entry address window (4 DWORDs / 128-bit per entry); physical depth DCT_DEPTH=16 (usable index 0-15)

4.3. Integration Limits

The SoC Management Interface header at 0x300 reports CAP_LENGTH = 0x1A DWORDs. This is inconsistent with the next capability header at 0x398: the span is 0x98 bytes, or 0x26 DWORDs. A generic walker that trusts the reported length advances to 0x368, inside the timing registers, and misinterprets data as a capability header. Software for this implementation must account for the fixed layout above rather than discovering that boundary from CAP_LENGTH.

The DAT and DCT capability fields advertise TABLE_SIZE = 127, but only 16 independent entries are implemented. Index decoding retains four bits, so higher window slots alias lower entries rather than providing extra storage. For example, DAT slot 16 at 0x480 aliases slot 0 at 0x400; DCT slot 16 at 0x900 aliases slot 0 at 0x800. Limit accesses to indexes 0–15. The complete windows in the generated map describe address decoding, not independent 128-entry capacity.

4.4. Address Alignment and Access

Address Alignment: * All registers are aligned to 32-bit boundaries (addresses are multiples of 4) * Effective address space per instance: 12 bits (0x000-0xFFF) * Instance spacing in multi-instance configurations: 0x1000 bytes

Access Width Requirements: * All registers must be accessed using 32-bit operations * Byte and halfword accesses are not supported * AXI4-Lite write strobe signals should be set to 4’b1111 for full-word writes

Endianness: * Data byte ordering: Little-endian (configurable via HC_CONTROL.DATA_BYTE_ORDER_MODE) * Bit numbering: Bit 0 is LSB, bit 31 is MSB * Multi-byte fields follow little-endian byte ordering

4.5. Programming Guidance

Configure queue thresholds and the DAT and DCT entries before setting HC_CONTROL.BUS_ENABLE. In a multi-controller system, check PRESENT_STATE.AC_CURRENT_OWN before submitting commands. When disabling the controller, wait until the controller no longer owns an active bus transaction.

The command port accepts one 64-bit descriptor as two consecutive 32-bit writes; the second write enqueues the descriptor. TX and RX data share offset 0x88, with writes targeting the TX FIFO and reads consuming the RX FIFO. Fill the TX FIFO before a write transaction begins, drain the RX and response FIFOs promptly, and use the queue-size and threshold fields rather than assuming a configured FIFO depth.

Each RESPONSE_PORT read returns a 32-bit response descriptor. Bits [15:0] contain the transferred data length, bits [23:16] are reserved, bits [27:24] contain the transaction ID, and bits [31:28] contain one of the following error status codes from TCRI 6.4.1:

Code RTL status Meaning

0x0

Success

Transaction completed without an error

0x1

Crc

CRC error

0x2

Parity

Parity error

0x3

Frame

Frame error

0x4

AddrHeader

Address-header error

0x5

Nack

Address or dynamic-address-assignment NACK

0x6

Ovl

Receive overflow or transmit underflow

0x7

I3cShortReadErr

Target returned fewer bytes than requested when a short read was not permitted

0x8

HcAborted

Host controller internal error or abort operation

0x9

I2cDataNackOrI3cBusAborted

I2C write-data NACK or I3C bus abort

0xA

NotSupported

Command is not supported

0xB

AbortedWithCRC

HDR-BT transaction aborted with a CRC error

0xC

ErrorC

Transfer-type-specific error

0xD

ErrorD

Transfer-type-specific error

0xE

ErrorE

Transfer-type-specific error

0xF

ErrorF

Transfer-type-specific error

Reset bits are self-clearing. Poll a requested reset until it reads as zero, and do not reset a queue while a transaction is active: doing so can discard queued commands, completion status, or transfer data. A soft reset resets controller state but does not restore the CSR configuration.

Host-controller status interrupts and PIO queue interrupts have separate status, enable, and signal-enable registers. Service every asserted status bit and clear write-one-to-clear fields as described in the generated register reference.

IBI data is padded to a 4-byte boundary. Use the data length from the IBI status descriptor to distinguish payload bytes from padding, and service the IBI queue before it overflows.

For standby-controller operation, configure STBY_CR_ENABLE_INIT and the required dynamic-address-assignment methods before enabling the bus. Send a controller-role request only while in standby mode and while the bus is idle.

How a bit is cleared depends on the bit:

- Auto-cleared by hardware on a specific access: RX_DESC_STAT, RX_DESC_THLD_STAT
and RX_DATA_THLD_STAT clear when software reads the corresponding RX queue; IBI_DONE
clears when software reads STATUS.LAST_IBI_STATUS. These bits are also write-1-to-clear,
but the normal servicing access clears them without an explicit write here.

- Write-1-to-clear only (no hardware auto-clear): TX_DESC_COMPLETE, TX_DESC_STAT,
TX_DESC_THLD_STAT, TX_DATA_THLD_STAT, IBI_THLD_STAT, and the not-implemented
TRANSFER_ERR_STAT, TRANSFER_ABORT_STAT, TX_DESC_TIMEOUT, RX_DESC_TIMEOUT. Write 1 to
the bit in this register to clear it.

- Read-only, hardware-driven level status that cannot be cleared by software:
PENDING_IBI and PENDING_INTERRUPT track live conditions.

0x1220i3c_csr[1].I3C_EC.TTI.INTERRUPT_STATUSRWInterrupt Status.

How a bit is cleared depends on the bit:

- Auto-cleared by hardware on a specific access: RX_DESC_STAT, RX_DESC_THLD_STAT
and RX_DATA_THLD_STAT clear when software reads the corresponding RX queue; IBI_DONE
clears when software reads STATUS.LAST_IBI_STATUS. These bits are also write-1-to-clear,
but the normal servicing access clears them without an explicit write here.

- Write-1-to-clear only (no hardware auto-clear): TX_DESC_COMPLETE, TX_DESC_STAT,
TX_DESC_THLD_STAT, TX_DATA_THLD_STAT, IBI_THLD_STAT, and the not-implemented
TRANSFER_ERR_STAT, TRANSFER_ABORT_STAT, TX_DESC_TIMEOUT, RX_DESC_TIMEOUT. Write 1 to
the bit in this register to clear it.

- Read-only, hardware-driven level status that cannot be cleared by software:
PENDING_IBI and PENDING_INTERRUPT track live conditions.

0x2220i3c_csr[2].I3C_EC.TTI.INTERRUPT_STATUSRWInterrupt Status.

How a bit is cleared depends on the bit:

- Auto-cleared by hardware on a specific access: RX_DESC_STAT, RX_DESC_THLD_STAT
and RX_DATA_THLD_STAT clear when software reads the corresponding RX queue; IBI_DONE
clears when software reads STATUS.LAST_IBI_STATUS. These bits are also write-1-to-clear,
but the normal servicing access clears them without an explicit write here.

- Write-1-to-clear only (no hardware auto-clear): TX_DESC_COMPLETE, TX_DESC_STAT,
TX_DESC_THLD_STAT, TX_DATA_THLD_STAT, IBI_THLD_STAT, and the not-implemented
TRANSFER_ERR_STAT, TRANSFER_ABORT_STAT, TX_DESC_TIMEOUT, RX_DESC_TIMEOUT. Write 1 to
the bit in this register to clear it.

- Read-only, hardware-driven level status that cannot be cleared by software:
PENDING_IBI and PENDING_INTERRUPT track live conditions.

0x3220i3c_csr[3].I3C_EC.TTI.INTERRUPT_STATUSRWInterrupt Status.

How a bit is cleared depends on the bit:

- Auto-cleared by hardware on a specific access: RX_DESC_STAT, RX_DESC_THLD_STAT
and RX_DATA_THLD_STAT clear when software reads the corresponding RX queue; IBI_DONE
clears when software reads STATUS.LAST_IBI_STATUS. These bits are also write-1-to-clear,
but the normal servicing access clears them without an explicit write here.

- Write-1-to-clear only (no hardware auto-clear): TX_DESC_COMPLETE, TX_DESC_STAT,
TX_DESC_THLD_STAT, TX_DATA_THLD_STAT, IBI_THLD_STAT, and the not-implemented
TRANSFER_ERR_STAT, TRANSFER_ABORT_STAT, TX_DESC_TIMEOUT, RX_DESC_TIMEOUT. Write 1 to
the bit in this register to clear it.

- Read-only, hardware-driven level status that cannot be cleared by software:
PENDING_IBI and PENDING_INTERRUPT track live conditions.

0x420Ci3c_csr[4].I3C_EC.TTI.RESET_CONTROLRWReset Control for Queues and IBI Retry Counter
0x4210i3c_csr[4].I3C_EC.TTI.QUEUE_STATUSRDynamic queue full/empty status for debug and monitoring
0x4214i3c_csr[4].I3C_EC.TTI.DESC_QUEUE_DEPTHRCurrent number of entries in descriptor queues
0x4218i3c_csr[4].I3C_EC.TTI.DATA_QUEUE_DEPTHRCurrent number of DWORD entries in data queues
0x421Ci3c_csr[4].I3C_EC.TTI.IBI_QUEUE_DEPTHRCurrent number of entries in IBI queue
0x4220i3c_csr[4].I3C_EC.TTI.INTERRUPT_STATUSRWInterrupt Status.

How a bit is cleared depends on the bit:

- Auto-cleared by hardware on a specific access: RX_DESC_STAT, RX_DESC_THLD_STAT
and RX_DATA_THLD_STAT clear when software reads the corresponding RX queue; IBI_DONE
clears when software reads STATUS.LAST_IBI_STATUS. These bits are also write-1-to-clear,
but the normal servicing access clears them without an explicit write here.

- Write-1-to-clear only (no hardware auto-clear): TX_DESC_COMPLETE, TX_DESC_STAT,
TX_DESC_THLD_STAT, TX_DATA_THLD_STAT, IBI_THLD_STAT, and the not-implemented
TRANSFER_ERR_STAT, TRANSFER_ABORT_STAT, TX_DESC_TIMEOUT, RX_DESC_TIMEOUT. Write 1 to
the bit in this register to clear it.

- Read-only, hardware-driven level status that cannot be cleared by software:
PENDING_IBI and PENDING_INTERRUPT track live conditions.

i3c_csr[0].PIOControl.IBI_PORT

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
31:0IBI_DATAR-Data Read from IBI Data Buffer.

The IBI Port is mapped to the IBI Data Queue.
IBI Data is always aligned to a 4-byte boundary and then put into the IBI Queue.
If the incoming data is not aligned to a 4-byte boundary,
then there will be extra (unused) bytes at the end of the transferred IBI data.
This can be determined from the value of field DATA_LENGTH in the IBI Status Descriptor.

i3c_csr[0].I3C_EC.SecFwRecoveryIf.PROT_CAP_2

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
31:16AGENT_CAPSRW0x0Agent capabilities:

* bit 0: Identification (DEVICE_ID structure)

* bit 1: Forced Recovery (From RESET)

* bit 2: Mgmt reset (From RESET)

* bit 3: Device Reset (From RESET)

* bit 4: Device status (DEVICE_STATUS)

* bit 5: Recovery memory access (INDIRECT_CTRL)

* bit 6: Local C-image support

* bit 7: Push C-image support

* bit 8: Interface isolation

* bit 9: Hardware status

* bit 10: Vendor command

* bit 11: Flashless boot (From RESET)

* bit 12: FIFO CMS support (INDIRECT_FIFO_CTRL)

* bits 13-15: Reserved
15:0REC_PROT_VERSIONRW0x0* Byte 0: Major version number = 0x1

* Byte 1: Minor version number = 0x1

i3c_csr[0].I3C_EC.SecFwRecoveryIf.DEVICE_STATUS_0

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
31:16REC_REASON_CODERW0x0* 0x0: No Boot Failure detected (BFNF)

* 0x1: Generic hardware error (BFGHWE)

* 0x2: Generic hardware soft error (BFGSE) - soft error may be recoverable

* 0x3: Self-test failure (BFSTF) (e.g., RSA self test failure, FIPs self test failure,, etc.)

* 0x4: Corrupted/missing critical data (BFCD)

* 0x5: Missing/corrupt key manifest (BFKMMC)

* 0x6: Authentication Failure on key manifest (BFKMAF)

* 0x7: Anti-rollback failure on key manifest (BFKIAR)

* 0x8: Missing/corrupt boot loader (first mutable code) firmware image (BFFIMC)

* 0x9: Authentication failure on boot loader ( 1st mutable code) firmware image (BFFIAF)

* 0xA: Anti-rollback failure boot loader (1st mutable code) firmware image (BFFIAR)

* 0xB: Missing/corrupt main/management firmware image (BFMFMC)

* 0xC: Authentication Failure main/management firmware image (BFMFAF)

* 0xD: Anti-rollback Failure main/management firmware image (BFMFAR)

* 0xE: Missing/corrupt recovery firmware (BFRFMC)

* 0xF: Authentication Failure recovery firmware (BFRFAF)

* 0x10: Anti-rollback Failure on recovery firmware (BFRFAR)

* 0x11: Forced Recovery (FR)

* 0x12: Flashless/Streaming Boot (FSB)

* 0x13-0x7F: Reserved

* 0x80-0xFF: Vendor Unique Boot Failure Codes
15:8PROT_ERRORRW0x0* 0x0: No Protocol Error

* 0x1: Unsupported/Write Command - command is not support or a write to a RO command

* 0x2: Unsupported Parameter

* 0x3: Length write error (length of write command is incorrect)

* 0x4: CRC Error (if supported)

* 0x5-0xFE: Reserved

* 0xFF: General Protocol Error - catch all unclassified errors
7:0DEV_STATUSRW0x0* 0x0: Status Pending (Recover Reason Code not populated)

* 0x1: Device healthy (Recover Reason Code not populated)

* 0x2: Device Error (“soft” error or other error state) - (Recover Reason Code not populated)

* 0x3: Recovery mode - ready to accept recovery image - (Recover Reason Code populated)

* 0x4: Recovery Pending (waiting for activation) - (Recover Reason Code populated)

* 0x5: Running Recovery Image ( Recover Reason Code not populated)

* 0x6-0xD: Reserved

* 0xE: Boot Failure (Recover Reason Code populated)

* 0xF: Fatal Error (Recover Reason Code not populated)

* 0x10-FF:Reserved

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
31:30STBY_CR_ENABLE_INITRW0x0Enables or disables the Secondary Controller:

2'b00 - DISABLED: Secondary Controller is disabled.

2'b01 - ACM_INIT: Secondary Controller is enabled,
but Host Controller initializes in Active Controller mode.

2'b10 - SCM_RUNNING: Secondary Controller operation is enabled,
Host Controller initializes in Standby Controller mode.

2'b11 - SCM_HOT_JOIN: Secondary Controller operation is enabled,
Host Controller conditionally becomes a Hot-Joining Device
to receive its Dynamic Address before operating in Standby Controller mode.

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
31RESET_DYNAMIC_ADDRRW0x1If set to 1'b1, then the Secondary Controller Logic must clear its Dynamic
Address in register STBY_CR_DEVICE_ADDR after receiving
a Target Reset Pattern that followed a Broadcast or Direct SET RSTACT CCC sent
to the Dynamic Address, with Defining Byte 0x01 or 0x02.
Requires support for Dynamic Address Assignment with at least one supported
method, such as the ENTDAA CCC, with field DAA_ENTDAA_ENABLE set to 1'b1 in
register STBY_CR_CONTROL.
If field ACR_FSM_OP_SELECT in register STBY_CR_CONTROL is set to 1'b1, then
this field shall be cleared (i.e., readiness to accept the Controller Role
shall be revoked) with this Target Reset Pattern.

i3c_csr[0].I3C_EC.TTI.CONTROL

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
31:16Reserved--Reserved
15:13IBI_RETRY_NUMRW0x0Number of times the Target Device will try to request an IBI before giving up.

Values:

0x0 - Device will never retry.

0x1-0x6 - Device will retry this many times.

0x7 - Device will retry indefinitely until the Active Controller sets
DISINT bit in the DISEC command.
12IBI_ENRW0x1Enable the IBI queue servicing.

Values:

0x0 - Device will not service the IBI queue.

0x1 - Device will send IBI requests onto the bus, if possible.
11CRR_ENRW0x0Enable Controller Role Request.

Values:

0x0 - Device is allowed to perform Controller Role Request.

0x1 - Device is not allowed to perform Controller Role Request.
10HJ_ENRW0x1Enable Hot-Join capability.

Values:

0x0 - Device is allowed to attempt Hot-Join.

0x1 - Device is not allowed to attempt Hot-Join.

i3c_csr[0].I3C_EC.TTI.STATUS

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
31:15Reserved--Reserved
14:12LAST_IBI_STATUSR0x0Status of last IBI. Should be read after IBI_DONE interrupt.

Values:

000 - Success: IBI was transmitted and ACK'd by the Active Controller.
001 - FailureNack: Active Controller NACK'd the IBI before any data was sent.
The device will retry sending the IBI as long as permitted by the retry counter.
010 - FailurePartialData: Active Controller NACK'd the IBI after data was
partially sent. The remaining part of data in the IBI queue is considered
corrupted and will be discarded.
011 - FailureRetry: IBI could not be serviced due to reached retry count.
100 - FailureArbitration:: IBI could not be serviced as arbitration was lost
during the address header. The device will retry sending the IBI as long as
permitted by the retry counter.
11:9Reserved--Reserved
8PROTOCOL_ERRORR0x0Protocol error occurred in the past. This field can only be reset
by the Controller, if it issues the GETSTATUS CCC.

Values:

0 - no error occurred

1 - generic protocol error occurred in the past. It will be set until reception
of the next GETSTATUS command.
7:0Reserved--Reserved

5IBI_QUEUE_RSTRW0x0TTI IBI Queue Buffer Software Reset
4RX_DATA_RSTRW0x0TTI RX Data Queue Buffer Software Reset
3TX_DATA_RSTRW0x0TTI TX Data Queue Buffer Software Reset. Also resets the tti_conv_Nto8 since first dword is immediately loaded into the converter and can't be cleared otherwise.

i3c_csr[0].I3C_EC.TTI.INTERRUPT_STATUS

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
31TRANSFER_ERR_STATRW0x0NOT IMPLEMENTED. Bus error occurred. Clear: write 1 to this bit in INTERRUPT_STATUS (no hardware auto-clear).
30:27Reserved--Reserved
26TX_DESC_COMPLETERW0x0Read Transaction on the I3C Bus completed. Clear: write 1 to this bit in INTERRUPT_STATUS (no hardware auto-clear).
25TRANSFER_ABORT_STATRW0x0NOT IMPLEMENTED. Bus aborted transaction. Clear: write 1 to this bit in INTERRUPT_STATUS (no hardware auto-clear).
24:21Reserved--Reserved
20PENDING_IBIR0x0At least one IBI is pending. Read-only, hardware-driven level status: it tracks the live IBI-pending condition and cannot be cleared by software (clears automatically when no IBI is pending).
19:16PENDING_INTERRUPTRW0x0Contains the interrupt number of any pending interrupt, or 0 if no interrupts are pending. This encoding allows for up to 15 numbered interrupts. If more than one interrupt is set, then the highest priority interrupt shall be returned. Read-only, hardware-driven level status: it cannot be cleared by software and updates as pending conditions change.
15:14Reserved--Reserved
13IBI_DONERW0x0IBI is done, check LAST_IBI_STATUS for result. Clear: auto-cleared by hardware when software reads STATUS.LAST_IBI_STATUS; writing 1 to this bit in INTERRUPT_STATUS also clears it.
12IBI_THLD_STATRW0x0TTI IBI Buffer Threshold Status, the Target Controller shall set this bit to 1 when the number of available entries in the TTI IBI Queue is >= the value defined in `TTI_IBI_THLD`. Clear: write 1 to this bit in INTERRUPT_STATUS (no hardware auto-clear).
11RX_DESC_THLD_STATRW0x0TTI RX Descriptor Buffer Threshold Status, the Target Controller shall set this bit to 1 when the number of available entries in the TTI RX Descriptor Queue is >= the value defined in `TTI_RX_DESC_THLD`. Clear: auto-cleared by hardware when software reads the RX Descriptor Queue; writing 1 to this bit in INTERRUPT_STATUS also clears it.
10TX_DESC_THLD_STATRW0x0TTI TX Descriptor Buffer Threshold Status, the Target Controller shall set this bit to 1 when the number of available entries in the TTI TX Descriptor Queue is >= the value defined in `TTI_TX_DESC_THLD`. Clear: write 1 to this bit in INTERRUPT_STATUS (no hardware auto-clear).
9RX_DATA_THLD_STATRW0x0TTI RX Data Buffer Threshold Status, the Target Controller shall set this bit to 1 when the number of entries in the TTI RX Data Queue is >= the value defined in `TTI_RX_DATA_THLD`. Clear: auto-cleared by hardware when software reads the RX Data Queue; writing 1 to this bit in INTERRUPT_STATUS also clears it.
8TX_DATA_THLD_STATRW0x0TTI TX Data Buffer Threshold Status, the Target Controller shall set this bit to 1 when the number of available entries in the TTI TX Data Queue is >= the value defined in `TTI_TX_DATA_THLD`. Clear: write 1 to this bit in INTERRUPT_STATUS (no hardware auto-clear).
7:4Reserved--Reserved
3TX_DESC_TIMEOUTRW0x0NOT IMPLEMENTED. Pending Read was NACK’ed, because the `TX_DESC_STAT` event was not handled in time. Clear: write 1 to this bit in INTERRUPT_STATUS (no hardware auto-clear).
2RX_DESC_TIMEOUTRW0x0NOT IMPLEMENTED. Pending Write was NACK’ed, because the `RX_DESC_STAT` event was not handled in time. Clear: write 1 to this bit in INTERRUPT_STATUS (no hardware auto-clear).
1TX_DESC_STATRW0x0There is a pending Read Transaction on the I3C Bus. Software should write data to the TX Descriptor Queue and the TX Data Queue. Clear: write 1 to this bit in INTERRUPT_STATUS (no hardware auto-clear — writing to the TX queues does not clear it).
0RX_DESC_STATRW0x0There is a pending Write Transaction. Software should read data from the RX Descriptor Queue and the RX Data Queue. Clear: auto-cleared by hardware when software reads the RX Descriptor Queue; writing 1 to this bit in INTERRUPT_STATUS also clears it.

i3c_csr[0].I3C_EC.SoCMgmtIf.HDR_TIMEOUT_EN_REG

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
31:1Reserved--Reserved
0HDR_TIMEOUT_ENRW0x0Enable bit for the optional 60us HDR error recovery timer (I3C spec 5.1.10.1.9). When enabled, the Target can recover from TE0/TE1 errors if both SCL and SDA stay High for a period exceeding the configured threshold. Disabled on reset.

i3c_csr[0].I3C_EC.SoCMgmtIf.T_HDR_TIMEOUT_REG

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
31:20Reserved--Reserved
19:0T_HDR_TIMEOUTRW0xEA60Timer threshold in clock cycles for the optional HDR error recovery (I3C spec 5.1.10.1.9). If both SCL and SDA stay High for this many clock cycles during a TE0/TE1 error-induced HDR mode, the Target exits HDR mode and returns to Idle. Configure based on System Clock to achieve 60us.

i3c_csr[1].PIOControl.IBI_PORT

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
31:0IBI_DATAR-Data Read from IBI Data Buffer.

The IBI Port is mapped to the IBI Data Queue.
IBI Data is always aligned to a 4-byte boundary and then put into the IBI Queue.
If the incoming data is not aligned to a 4-byte boundary,
then there will be extra (unused) bytes at the end of the transferred IBI data.
This can be determined from the value of field DATA_LENGTH in the IBI Status Descriptor.

i3c_csr[1].I3C_EC.SecFwRecoveryIf.PROT_CAP_2

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
31:16AGENT_CAPSRW0x0Agent capabilities:

* bit 0: Identification (DEVICE_ID structure)

* bit 1: Forced Recovery (From RESET)

* bit 2: Mgmt reset (From RESET)

* bit 3: Device Reset (From RESET)

* bit 4: Device status (DEVICE_STATUS)

* bit 5: Recovery memory access (INDIRECT_CTRL)

* bit 6: Local C-image support

* bit 7: Push C-image support

* bit 8: Interface isolation

* bit 9: Hardware status

* bit 10: Vendor command

* bit 11: Flashless boot (From RESET)

* bit 12: FIFO CMS support (INDIRECT_FIFO_CTRL)

* bits 13-15: Reserved
15:0REC_PROT_VERSIONRW0x0* Byte 0: Major version number = 0x1

* Byte 1: Minor version number = 0x1

i3c_csr[1].I3C_EC.SecFwRecoveryIf.DEVICE_STATUS_0

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
31:16REC_REASON_CODERW0x0* 0x0: No Boot Failure detected (BFNF)

* 0x1: Generic hardware error (BFGHWE)

* 0x2: Generic hardware soft error (BFGSE) - soft error may be recoverable

* 0x3: Self-test failure (BFSTF) (e.g., RSA self test failure, FIPs self test failure,, etc.)

* 0x4: Corrupted/missing critical data (BFCD)

* 0x5: Missing/corrupt key manifest (BFKMMC)

* 0x6: Authentication Failure on key manifest (BFKMAF)

* 0x7: Anti-rollback failure on key manifest (BFKIAR)

* 0x8: Missing/corrupt boot loader (first mutable code) firmware image (BFFIMC)

* 0x9: Authentication failure on boot loader ( 1st mutable code) firmware image (BFFIAF)

* 0xA: Anti-rollback failure boot loader (1st mutable code) firmware image (BFFIAR)

* 0xB: Missing/corrupt main/management firmware image (BFMFMC)

* 0xC: Authentication Failure main/management firmware image (BFMFAF)

* 0xD: Anti-rollback Failure main/management firmware image (BFMFAR)

* 0xE: Missing/corrupt recovery firmware (BFRFMC)

* 0xF: Authentication Failure recovery firmware (BFRFAF)

* 0x10: Anti-rollback Failure on recovery firmware (BFRFAR)

* 0x11: Forced Recovery (FR)

* 0x12: Flashless/Streaming Boot (FSB)

* 0x13-0x7F: Reserved

* 0x80-0xFF: Vendor Unique Boot Failure Codes
15:8PROT_ERRORRW0x0* 0x0: No Protocol Error

* 0x1: Unsupported/Write Command - command is not support or a write to a RO command

* 0x2: Unsupported Parameter

* 0x3: Length write error (length of write command is incorrect)

* 0x4: CRC Error (if supported)

* 0x5-0xFE: Reserved

* 0xFF: General Protocol Error - catch all unclassified errors
7:0DEV_STATUSRW0x0* 0x0: Status Pending (Recover Reason Code not populated)

* 0x1: Device healthy (Recover Reason Code not populated)

* 0x2: Device Error (“soft” error or other error state) - (Recover Reason Code not populated)

* 0x3: Recovery mode - ready to accept recovery image - (Recover Reason Code populated)

* 0x4: Recovery Pending (waiting for activation) - (Recover Reason Code populated)

* 0x5: Running Recovery Image ( Recover Reason Code not populated)

* 0x6-0xD: Reserved

* 0xE: Boot Failure (Recover Reason Code populated)

* 0xF: Fatal Error (Recover Reason Code not populated)

* 0x10-FF:Reserved

31:30STBY_CR_ENABLE_INITRW0x0Enables or disables the Secondary Controller:

2'b00 - DISABLED: Secondary Controller is disabled.

2'b01 - ACM_INIT: Secondary Controller is enabled,
but Host Controller initializes in Active Controller mode.

2'b10 - SCM_RUNNING: Secondary Controller operation is enabled,
Host Controller initializes in Standby Controller mode.

2'b11 - SCM_HOT_JOIN: Secondary Controller operation is enabled,
Host Controller conditionally becomes a Hot-Joining Device
to receive its Dynamic Address before operating in Standby Controller mode.

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
31RESET_DYNAMIC_ADDRRW0x1If set to 1'b1, then the Secondary Controller Logic must clear its Dynamic
Address in register STBY_CR_DEVICE_ADDR after receiving
a Target Reset Pattern that followed a Broadcast or Direct SET RSTACT CCC sent
to the Dynamic Address, with Defining Byte 0x01 or 0x02.
Requires support for Dynamic Address Assignment with at least one supported
method, such as the ENTDAA CCC, with field DAA_ENTDAA_ENABLE set to 1'b1 in
register STBY_CR_CONTROL.
If field ACR_FSM_OP_SELECT in register STBY_CR_CONTROL is set to 1'b1, then
this field shall be cleared (i.e., readiness to accept the Controller Role
shall be revoked) with this Target Reset Pattern.

Values:

i3c_csr[1].I3C_EC.TTI.STATUS

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
31:15Reserved--Reserved
14:12LAST_IBI_STATUSR0x0Status of last IBI. Should be read after IBI_DONE interrupt.

Values:

000 - Success: IBI was transmitted and ACK'd by the Active Controller.
001 - FailureNack: Active Controller NACK'd the IBI before any data was sent.
The device will retry sending the IBI as long as permitted by the retry counter.
010 - FailurePartialData: Active Controller NACK'd the IBI after data was
partially sent. The remaining part of data in the IBI queue is considered
corrupted and will be discarded.
011 - FailureRetry: IBI could not be serviced due to reached retry count.
100 - FailureArbitration:: IBI could not be serviced as arbitration was lost
during the address header. The device will retry sending the IBI as long as
permitted by the retry counter.
11:9Reserved--Reserved
8PROTOCOL_ERRORR0x0Protocol error occurred in the past. This field can only be reset
by the Controller, if it issues the GETSTATUS CCC.

Values:

0 - no error occurred

1 - generic protocol error occurred in the past. It will be set until reception
of the next GETSTATUS command.
7:0Reserved--Reserved

3TX_DATA_RSTRW0x0TTI TX Data Queue Buffer Software Reset. Also resets the tti_conv_Nto8 since first dword is immediately loaded into the converter and can't be cleared otherwise.
2RX_DESC_RSTRW0x0TTI RX Descriptor Queue Buffer Software Reset
1TX_DESC_RSTRW0x0TTI TX Descriptor Queue Buffer Software Reset
0SOFT_RSTRW0x0Target Core Software Reset

i3c_csr[1].I3C_EC.TTI.INTERRUPT_STATUS

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
31TRANSFER_ERR_STATRW0x0NOT IMPLEMENTED. Bus error occurred. Clear: write 1 to this bit in INTERRUPT_STATUS (no hardware auto-clear).
30:27Reserved--Reserved
26TX_DESC_COMPLETERW0x0Read Transaction on the I3C Bus completed. Clear: write 1 to this bit in INTERRUPT_STATUS (no hardware auto-clear).
25TRANSFER_ABORT_STATRW0x0NOT IMPLEMENTED. Bus aborted transaction. Clear: write 1 to this bit in INTERRUPT_STATUS (no hardware auto-clear).
24:21Reserved--Reserved
20PENDING_IBIR0x0At least one IBI is pending. Read-only, hardware-driven level status: it tracks the live IBI-pending condition and cannot be cleared by software (clears automatically when no IBI is pending).
19:16PENDING_INTERRUPTRW0x0Contains the interrupt number of any pending interrupt, or 0 if no interrupts are pending. This encoding allows for up to 15 numbered interrupts. If more than one interrupt is set, then the highest priority interrupt shall be returned. Read-only, hardware-driven level status: it cannot be cleared by software and updates as pending conditions change.
15:14Reserved--Reserved
13IBI_DONERW0x0IBI is done, check LAST_IBI_STATUS for result. Clear: auto-cleared by hardware when software reads STATUS.LAST_IBI_STATUS; writing 1 to this bit in INTERRUPT_STATUS also clears it.
12IBI_THLD_STATRW0x0TTI IBI Buffer Threshold Status, the Target Controller shall set this bit to 1 when the number of available entries in the TTI IBI Queue is >= the value defined in `TTI_IBI_THLD`. Clear: write 1 to this bit in INTERRUPT_STATUS (no hardware auto-clear).
11RX_DESC_THLD_STATRW0x0TTI RX Descriptor Buffer Threshold Status, the Target Controller shall set this bit to 1 when the number of available entries in the TTI RX Descriptor Queue is >= the value defined in `TTI_RX_DESC_THLD`. Clear: auto-cleared by hardware when software reads the RX Descriptor Queue; writing 1 to this bit in INTERRUPT_STATUS also clears it.
10TX_DESC_THLD_STATRW0x0TTI TX Descriptor Buffer Threshold Status, the Target Controller shall set this bit to 1 when the number of available entries in the TTI TX Descriptor Queue is >= the value defined in `TTI_TX_DESC_THLD`. Clear: write 1 to this bit in INTERRUPT_STATUS (no hardware auto-clear).
9RX_DATA_THLD_STATRW0x0TTI RX Data Buffer Threshold Status, the Target Controller shall set this bit to 1 when the number of entries in the TTI RX Data Queue is >= the value defined in `TTI_RX_DATA_THLD`. Clear: auto-cleared by hardware when software reads the RX Data Queue; writing 1 to this bit in INTERRUPT_STATUS also clears it.
8TX_DATA_THLD_STATRW0x0TTI TX Data Buffer Threshold Status, the Target Controller shall set this bit to 1 when the number of available entries in the TTI TX Data Queue is >= the value defined in `TTI_TX_DATA_THLD`. Clear: write 1 to this bit in INTERRUPT_STATUS (no hardware auto-clear).
7:4Reserved--Reserved
3TX_DESC_TIMEOUTRW0x0NOT IMPLEMENTED. Pending Read was NACK’ed, because the `TX_DESC_STAT` event was not handled in time. Clear: write 1 to this bit in INTERRUPT_STATUS (no hardware auto-clear).
2RX_DESC_TIMEOUTRW0x0NOT IMPLEMENTED. Pending Write was NACK’ed, because the `RX_DESC_STAT` event was not handled in time. Clear: write 1 to this bit in INTERRUPT_STATUS (no hardware auto-clear).
1TX_DESC_STATRW0x0There is a pending Read Transaction on the I3C Bus. Software should write data to the TX Descriptor Queue and the TX Data Queue. Clear: write 1 to this bit in INTERRUPT_STATUS (no hardware auto-clear — writing to the TX queues does not clear it).
0RX_DESC_STATRW0x0There is a pending Write Transaction. Software should read data from the RX Descriptor Queue and the RX Data Queue. Clear: auto-cleared by hardware when software reads the RX Descriptor Queue; writing 1 to this bit in INTERRUPT_STATUS also clears it.

i3c_csr[1].I3C_EC.SoCMgmtIf.HDR_TIMEOUT_EN_REG

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
31:1Reserved--Reserved
0HDR_TIMEOUT_ENRW0x0Enable bit for the optional 60us HDR error recovery timer (I3C spec 5.1.10.1.9). When enabled, the Target can recover from TE0/TE1 errors if both SCL and SDA stay High for a period exceeding the configured threshold. Disabled on reset.

i3c_csr[1].I3C_EC.SoCMgmtIf.T_HDR_TIMEOUT_REG

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
31:20Reserved--Reserved
19:0T_HDR_TIMEOUTRW0xEA60Timer threshold in clock cycles for the optional HDR error recovery (I3C spec 5.1.10.1.9). If both SCL and SDA stay High for this many clock cycles during a TE0/TE1 error-induced HDR mode, the Target exits HDR mode and returns to Idle. Configure based on System Clock to achieve 60us.

i3c_csr[2].PIOControl.IBI_PORT

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
31:0IBI_DATAR-Data Read from IBI Data Buffer.

The IBI Port is mapped to the IBI Data Queue.
IBI Data is always aligned to a 4-byte boundary and then put into the IBI Queue.
If the incoming data is not aligned to a 4-byte boundary,
then there will be extra (unused) bytes at the end of the transferred IBI data.
This can be determined from the value of field DATA_LENGTH in the IBI Status Descriptor.

i3c_csr[2].I3C_EC.SecFwRecoveryIf.PROT_CAP_2

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
31:16AGENT_CAPSRW0x0Agent capabilities:

* bit 0: Identification (DEVICE_ID structure)

* bit 1: Forced Recovery (From RESET)

* bit 2: Mgmt reset (From RESET)

* bit 3: Device Reset (From RESET)

* bit 4: Device status (DEVICE_STATUS)

* bit 5: Recovery memory access (INDIRECT_CTRL)

* bit 6: Local C-image support

* bit 7: Push C-image support

* bit 8: Interface isolation

* bit 9: Hardware status

* bit 10: Vendor command

* bit 11: Flashless boot (From RESET)

* bit 12: FIFO CMS support (INDIRECT_FIFO_CTRL)

* bits 13-15: Reserved
15:0REC_PROT_VERSIONRW0x0* Byte 0: Major version number = 0x1

* Byte 1: Minor version number = 0x1

i3c_csr[2].I3C_EC.SecFwRecoveryIf.DEVICE_STATUS_0

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
31:16REC_REASON_CODERW0x0* 0x0: No Boot Failure detected (BFNF)

* 0x1: Generic hardware error (BFGHWE)

* 0x2: Generic hardware soft error (BFGSE) - soft error may be recoverable

* 0x3: Self-test failure (BFSTF) (e.g., RSA self test failure, FIPs self test failure,, etc.)

* 0x4: Corrupted/missing critical data (BFCD)

* 0x5: Missing/corrupt key manifest (BFKMMC)

* 0x6: Authentication Failure on key manifest (BFKMAF)

* 0x7: Anti-rollback failure on key manifest (BFKIAR)

* 0x8: Missing/corrupt boot loader (first mutable code) firmware image (BFFIMC)

* 0x9: Authentication failure on boot loader ( 1st mutable code) firmware image (BFFIAF)

* 0xA: Anti-rollback failure boot loader (1st mutable code) firmware image (BFFIAR)

* 0xB: Missing/corrupt main/management firmware image (BFMFMC)

* 0xC: Authentication Failure main/management firmware image (BFMFAF)

* 0xD: Anti-rollback Failure main/management firmware image (BFMFAR)

* 0xE: Missing/corrupt recovery firmware (BFRFMC)

* 0xF: Authentication Failure recovery firmware (BFRFAF)

* 0x10: Anti-rollback Failure on recovery firmware (BFRFAR)

* 0x11: Forced Recovery (FR)

* 0x12: Flashless/Streaming Boot (FSB)

* 0x13-0x7F: Reserved

* 0x80-0xFF: Vendor Unique Boot Failure Codes
15:8PROT_ERRORRW0x0* 0x0: No Protocol Error

* 0x1: Unsupported/Write Command - command is not support or a write to a RO command

* 0x2: Unsupported Parameter

* 0x3: Length write error (length of write command is incorrect)

* 0x4: CRC Error (if supported)

* 0x5-0xFE: Reserved

* 0xFF: General Protocol Error - catch all unclassified errors
7:0DEV_STATUSRW0x0* 0x0: Status Pending (Recover Reason Code not populated)

* 0x1: Device healthy (Recover Reason Code not populated)

* 0x2: Device Error (“soft” error or other error state) - (Recover Reason Code not populated)

* 0x3: Recovery mode - ready to accept recovery image - (Recover Reason Code populated)

* 0x4: Recovery Pending (waiting for activation) - (Recover Reason Code populated)

* 0x5: Running Recovery Image ( Recover Reason Code not populated)

* 0x6-0xD: Reserved

* 0xE: Boot Failure (Recover Reason Code populated)

* 0xF: Fatal Error (Recover Reason Code not populated)

* 0x10-FF:Reserved

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
31:30STBY_CR_ENABLE_INITRW0x0Enables or disables the Secondary Controller:

2'b00 - DISABLED: Secondary Controller is disabled.

2'b01 - ACM_INIT: Secondary Controller is enabled,
but Host Controller initializes in Active Controller mode.

2'b10 - SCM_RUNNING: Secondary Controller operation is enabled,
Host Controller initializes in Standby Controller mode.

2'b11 - SCM_HOT_JOIN: Secondary Controller operation is enabled,
Host Controller conditionally becomes a Hot-Joining Device
to receive its Dynamic Address before operating in Standby Controller mode.

i3c_csr[2].I3C_EC.StdbyCtrlMode.STBY_CR_CCC_CONFIG_RSTACT_PARAMS

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
31RESET_DYNAMIC_ADDRRW0x1If set to 1'b1, then the Secondary Controller Logic must clear its Dynamic
Address in register STBY_CR_DEVICE_ADDR after receiving
a Target Reset Pattern that followed a Broadcast or Direct SET RSTACT CCC sent
to the Dynamic Address, with Defining Byte 0x01 or 0x02.
Requires support for Dynamic Address Assignment with at least one supported
method, such as the ENTDAA CCC, with field DAA_ENTDAA_ENABLE set to 1'b1 in
register STBY_CR_CONTROL.
If field ACR_FSM_OP_SELECT in register STBY_CR_CONTROL is set to 1'b1, then
this field shall be cleared (i.e., readiness to accept the Controller Role
shall be revoked) with this Target Reset Pattern.
30:24Reserved--Reserved
23:16RESET_TIME_TARGETRW0x0For Direct GET CCC, this field is returned for Defining Byte 0x82.
15:8RESET_TIME_PERIPHERALRW0x0For Direct GET CCC, this field is returned for Defining Byte 0x81.
7:0RST_ACTIONR0x1Contains the Defining Byte received with the last Direct SET CCC sent by the Active Controller. If not armed (START or RSTACT with unsupported defining byte) set to spec default 0x1.

15:13IBI_RETRY_NUMRW0x0Number of times the Target Device will try to request an IBI before giving up.

Values:

0x0 - Device will never retry.

0x1-0x6 - Device will retry this many times.

0x7 - Device will retry indefinitely until the Active Controller sets
DISINT bit in the DISEC command.
12IBI_ENRW0x1Enable the IBI queue servicing.

Values:

0x0 - Device will not service the IBI queue.

0x1 - Device will send IBI requests onto the bus, if possible.
11CRR_ENRW0x0Enable Controller Role Request.

Values:

0x0 - Device is allowed to perform Controller Role Request.

0x1 - Device is not allowed to perform Controller Role Request.
10HJ_ENRW0x1Enable Hot-Join capability.

Values:

0x0 - Device is allowed to attempt Hot-Join.

0x1 - Device is not allowed to attempt Hot-Join.
9:0Reserved--Reserved

i3c_csr[2].I3C_EC.TTI.STATUS

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
31:15Reserved--Reserved
14:12LAST_IBI_STATUSR0x0Status of last IBI. Should be read after IBI_DONE interrupt.

Values:

000 - Success: IBI was transmitted and ACK'd by the Active Controller.
001 - FailureNack: Active Controller NACK'd the IBI before any data was sent.
The device will retry sending the IBI as long as permitted by the retry counter.
010 - FailurePartialData: Active Controller NACK'd the IBI after data was
partially sent. The remaining part of data in the IBI queue is considered
corrupted and will be discarded.
011 - FailureRetry: IBI could not be serviced due to reached retry count.
100 - FailureArbitration:: IBI could not be serviced as arbitration was lost
during the address header. The device will retry sending the IBI as long as
permitted by the retry counter.
11:9Reserved--Reserved
8PROTOCOL_ERRORR0x0Protocol error occurred in the past. This field can only be reset
by the Controller, if it issues the GETSTATUS CCC.

Values:

0 - no error occurred

1 - generic protocol error occurred in the past. It will be set until reception
of the next GETSTATUS command.
7:0Reserved--Reserved

i3c_csr[2].I3C_EC.TTI.INTERRUPT_STATUS

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
31TRANSFER_ERR_STATRW0x0NOT IMPLEMENTED. Bus error occurred. Clear: write 1 to this bit in INTERRUPT_STATUS (no hardware auto-clear).
30:27Reserved--Reserved
26TX_DESC_COMPLETERW0x0Read Transaction on the I3C Bus completed. Clear: write 1 to this bit in INTERRUPT_STATUS (no hardware auto-clear).
25TRANSFER_ABORT_STATRW0x0NOT IMPLEMENTED. Bus aborted transaction. Clear: write 1 to this bit in INTERRUPT_STATUS (no hardware auto-clear).
24:21Reserved--Reserved
20PENDING_IBIR0x0At least one IBI is pending. Read-only, hardware-driven level status: it tracks the live IBI-pending condition and cannot be cleared by software (clears automatically when no IBI is pending).
19:16PENDING_INTERRUPTRW0x0Contains the interrupt number of any pending interrupt, or 0 if no interrupts are pending. This encoding allows for up to 15 numbered interrupts. If more than one interrupt is set, then the highest priority interrupt shall be returned. Read-only, hardware-driven level status: it cannot be cleared by software and updates as pending conditions change.
15:14Reserved--Reserved
13IBI_DONERW0x0IBI is done, check LAST_IBI_STATUS for result. Clear: auto-cleared by hardware when software reads STATUS.LAST_IBI_STATUS; writing 1 to this bit in INTERRUPT_STATUS also clears it.
12IBI_THLD_STATRW0x0TTI IBI Buffer Threshold Status, the Target Controller shall set this bit to 1 when the number of available entries in the TTI IBI Queue is >= the value defined in `TTI_IBI_THLD`. Clear: write 1 to this bit in INTERRUPT_STATUS (no hardware auto-clear).
11RX_DESC_THLD_STATRW0x0TTI RX Descriptor Buffer Threshold Status, the Target Controller shall set this bit to 1 when the number of available entries in the TTI RX Descriptor Queue is >= the value defined in `TTI_RX_DESC_THLD`. Clear: auto-cleared by hardware when software reads the RX Descriptor Queue; writing 1 to this bit in INTERRUPT_STATUS also clears it.
10TX_DESC_THLD_STATRW0x0TTI TX Descriptor Buffer Threshold Status, the Target Controller shall set this bit to 1 when the number of available entries in the TTI TX Descriptor Queue is >= the value defined in `TTI_TX_DESC_THLD`. Clear: write 1 to this bit in INTERRUPT_STATUS (no hardware auto-clear).
9RX_DATA_THLD_STATRW0x0TTI RX Data Buffer Threshold Status, the Target Controller shall set this bit to 1 when the number of entries in the TTI RX Data Queue is >= the value defined in `TTI_RX_DATA_THLD`. Clear: auto-cleared by hardware when software reads the RX Data Queue; writing 1 to this bit in INTERRUPT_STATUS also clears it.
8TX_DATA_THLD_STATRW0x0TTI TX Data Buffer Threshold Status, the Target Controller shall set this bit to 1 when the number of available entries in the TTI TX Data Queue is >= the value defined in `TTI_TX_DATA_THLD`. Clear: write 1 to this bit in INTERRUPT_STATUS (no hardware auto-clear).
7:4Reserved--Reserved
3TX_DESC_TIMEOUTRW0x0NOT IMPLEMENTED. Pending Read was NACK’ed, because the `TX_DESC_STAT` event was not handled in time. Clear: write 1 to this bit in INTERRUPT_STATUS (no hardware auto-clear).
2RX_DESC_TIMEOUTRW0x0NOT IMPLEMENTED. Pending Write was NACK’ed, because the `RX_DESC_STAT` event was not handled in time. Clear: write 1 to this bit in INTERRUPT_STATUS (no hardware auto-clear).
1TX_DESC_STATRW0x0There is a pending Read Transaction on the I3C Bus. Software should write data to the TX Descriptor Queue and the TX Data Queue. Clear: write 1 to this bit in INTERRUPT_STATUS (no hardware auto-clear — writing to the TX queues does not clear it).
0RX_DESC_STATRW0x0There is a pending Write Transaction. Software should read data from the RX Descriptor Queue and the RX Data Queue. Clear: auto-cleared by hardware when software reads the RX Descriptor Queue; writing 1 to this bit in INTERRUPT_STATUS also clears it.

i3c_csr[2].I3C_EC.SoCMgmtIf.HDR_TIMEOUT_EN_REG

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
31:1Reserved--Reserved
0HDR_TIMEOUT_ENRW0x0Enable bit for the optional 60us HDR error recovery timer (I3C spec 5.1.10.1.9). When enabled, the Target can recover from TE0/TE1 errors if both SCL and SDA stay High for a period exceeding the configured threshold. Disabled on reset.

i3c_csr[2].I3C_EC.SoCMgmtIf.T_HDR_TIMEOUT_REG

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
31:20Reserved--Reserved
19:0T_HDR_TIMEOUTRW0xEA60Timer threshold in clock cycles for the optional HDR error recovery (I3C spec 5.1.10.1.9). If both SCL and SDA stay High for this many clock cycles during a TE0/TE1 error-induced HDR mode, the Target exits HDR mode and returns to Idle. Configure based on System Clock to achieve 60us.

i3c_csr[3].PIOControl.IBI_PORT

BitsFieldAccessResetDescription
31:0IBI_DATAR-Data Read from IBI Data Buffer.

The IBI Port is mapped to the IBI Data Queue.
IBI Data is always aligned to a 4-byte boundary and then put into the IBI Queue.
If the incoming data is not aligned to a 4-byte boundary,
then there will be extra (unused) bytes at the end of the transferred IBI data.
This can be determined from the value of field DATA_LENGTH in the IBI Status Descriptor.

1 - Request PIO Abort,
0 - Resume PIO execution
1RSRW0x0Run/Stop execution of enqueued commands.
When set to 0, it holds execution of enqueued commands and runs current command to completion.
1 - PIO Queue start request,
0 - PIO Queue stop request.
0ENABLERW0x1Enables PIO queues. When disabled, SW may not read from/write to PIO queues.
1 - PIO queue enable request,
0 - PIO queue disable request

31:16AGENT_CAPSRW0x0Agent capabilities:

* bit 0: Identification (DEVICE_ID structure)

* bit 1: Forced Recovery (From RESET)

* bit 8: Interface isolation
